| Tsukasa | Now, where shall I enjoy my most elegant luncheon? Hm? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Ooh, I behold a breezy place illuminated by sunbeams filtered through the foliage. It is as if it's a natural spotlight made to shine on me! | talk |
| Tsukasa | Perfect. The perfect place for my most elegant luncheon. I'm impressed by myself for finding this amazing place! | talk |
| Tsukasa | Now, a handkerchief to prevent my uniform from getting dirty... | talk |
| Tsukasa | Hmph, how very gentlemanly of me. | talk |
| Tsukasa | And now it is time to enjoy my elegant luncheon...<br>Hm? What is this little black dot in the air... | talk |
| Tsukasa | I can't really see because it's too small—GAAAAAAH!!! | talk |
|  | bgm00000 | Sound |
| Tsukasa | S-S-S-Spider?! | talk |
|  | bgm00003 | Sound |
| Tsukasa | Stay away! Stay away from me! I found this place first! There are so many other trees here you can climb on! Get away from me! | talk |

| Tsukasa | D-Drats... What should I do?! | talk |
| Akito | Oh, that place under the tree looks like a nice place for a nap. Huh, someone's already there? | talk |

| Akito | Tsukasa! What are you doing over there?<br>And why're you all hunched up like that? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Hunched up?! How can you say that, when I'm so valiantly trying to confront this unscrupulous spider preventing me from enjoying my luncheon?! | talk |
| Akito | Spider? You're afraid of spiders? | talk |
| Tsukasa | N-Not at all! I'm just trying to prevent this infernal bug from contaminating my precious handkerchief... | talk |
| Akito | Handkerchief?<br>Where's the spider, anyway? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Right there! Look, it's just over there! | talk |
| Akito | I don't see anything. | talk |
| Tsukasa | You're not looking hard enough! Look again! | talk |
| Akito | Oh, there it is. | talk |
| Tsukasa | See? Look at how it's swaying around like it owns the place! | talk |
| Akito | It's just a small little dot you can hardly see, there's no need to be so bothered by— | talk |
| Tsukasa | Of course I'm bothered! A small spider is still a spider! | talk |
| Akito | (Sigh... I was gonna just tease him a little, but it's kinda anticlimactic when he's already shrieking like that.) | talk |
| Akito | You just want it to move somewhere else, right?<br>Caught it. | talk |

| Akito | There, I put it in the bush over there.<br>It's gone now. | talk |

| Tsukasa | Y-You saved me... | talk |

| Tsukasa | Hey, wait! Allow me to share some of my luncheon with you as a token of appreciation for saving me from that terror. | talk |

| Tsukasa | Luncheon, you plebeian!<br>Anyway, go ahead and take any side dish that catches your fancy. | talk |
| Akito | Okay, if you insist... | talk |

| Akito | I'll take this cheesecake, then. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Ugh, I was going to have that as dessert after the meal... But, it's fine. I did say it was a token of appreciation. | talk |
| Akito | Thanks. | talk |

| Tsukasa | Savor it slowly, understand? | talk |
| Akito | Yeah, yeah. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Just say it once! | talk |

| Tsukasa | I'm counting on you! | talk |

| Mizuki's Voice | Hm? Hey, Tsukasa...! | talk |
| Tsukasa | You're Rui's friend who sat in the front row at my play with Toya and Akito! | talk |
| Tsukasa | What was your name again? Akiyama? | talk |
| Mizuki | Yup, that's me♪ | talk |
| Tsukasa | I still vividly remember you bursting into tears laughing during the show! | talk |
| Tsukasa | It wasn't really meant to make people laugh, but it's always rewarding to see the audience having a good time! | talk |
| Mizuki | What, you mean it? I thought I might've been pretty obnoxious, but I couldn't help myself...! | talk |
| Tsukasa | You should never be ashamed of reacting so naturally. They're what makes a play complete. | talk |
| Mizuki | Huh? Care to explain? | talk |
| Tsukasa | A theatrical performance involves two groups of people: the actors on the stage and the audience watching them. | talk |
| Tsukasa | The actors assume their roles and create a world for the audience to behold and be drawn into! | talk |
| Tsukasa | The audience's reactions, such as laughter, tears or anger, help them become a part of the play too. | talk |

| Nene | Yeah, I'm glad we saw it since I'd been meaning to go see that play. | talk |
| Tsukasa | I'm also glad. I didn't know what to do when I heard they'd immediately sold out of tickets. And then our friend from Shunmeiza said they'd give us some... | talk |
| Rui | Thank goodness it turned out that their friend is a member of the troupe we saw perform today. I suppose it speaks to the importance of networking. | talk |
| Emu | And the story was so good☆ | talk |
| Emu | I loved how the protagonist didn't know he was a ghost while talking to his friends! | talk |
| Rui | Those around him knew that he'd become a ghost, which led to some comical misunderstandings during their conversations. The audience certainly seemed to have a nice laugh. | talk |
| Nene | I hadn't laughed that hard at a play in such a long time. | talk |
| Nene | But the scene when the protagonist departs into the afterlife was kinda sad. | talk |
| Emu | Oh, I felt the same way! | talk |
| Emu | The way he told his friends that they'd always been smiling before then, and how he wanted them to still be smiling when they said goodbye... It really made me tear up... | talk |
| Rui | Yes, that was a wonderful climax.<br>The expression the protagonist made as he held back his tears felt so compelling. | talk |
| Rui | What did you think, Tsukasa? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Me...? Yes, I also thought the final scene was very good. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Even the minor characters who were in that scene... They also gave excellent performances. | talk |
| Nene | The minor characters? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes. Remember how I also played a minor role during the play for newcomers? I put in quite a bit of effort for that. | talk |
| Tsukasa | So, I tried learning what I could by watching all the characters like that! | talk |
| Nene | I see. | talk |
| Tsukasa | But... I felt most drawn in by the protagonist's uncle. | talk |
| Nene | His uncle...?<br>Oh, the person off to the side during the funeral and the final scene? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes. He didn't have all that many lines since he wasn't close with the protagonist... | talk |
| Tsukasa | But the expressions he made forced me to think about what he was feeling deep down. | talk |
| Rui | Deep down? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes. For example, when the protagonist departed into the afterlife... | talk |
| Tsukasa | Until that point, his uncle had been seeing him as a ghost. But then, right as the protagonist was about to disappear, he moved his foot forward ever so slightly. | talk |
| Tsukasa | And just for that split second, the look in the uncle's eyes changed. It almost felt like he was the protagonist's father... | talk |
| Nene | His father...? | talk |
| Tsukasa | That was the moment I realized just how much the protagonist meant to him. | talk |
| Tsukasa | There may've been a time when he really did feel like a son. When he was little, the protagonist might've spent a lot of time playing with his dear uncle. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Of course, this is just my imagination talking. None of these things were depicted during the show... | talk |
| Rui | I see...<br>The acting performance put on by that minor character's actor made you think about his backstory. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, because he added so much depth to the story without speaking. And he did so without negatively impacting the rest of the scene... | talk |
| Tsukasa | It seemed like a good example to refer to while I try to work on thinking about the performance as a whole while maintaining my fervent passion for acting. | talk |
| Rui | I see. | talk |
| Rui | I didn't realize how educational this was for you. | talk |
| Tsukasa | It was. And we need to once more thank the person who gave us these tickets! | talk |
| Nene | Right. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Still, in the end, I couldn't help but see how the entire audience was smiling after it was all over. | talk |
| Rui | Me too. I suppose it's only natural since it included quite a lot of comedic elements... | talk |
| Rui | And it was clear that the cast wanted to send the audience home smiling, which they succeeded in doing. | talk |
| Emu | I felt that too!<br>It's like they were all doing everything they could to make the audience smile and laugh! | talk |
| Emu | Plus, the way the whole cast was smiling in the end made me really happy♪ | talk |
| Tsukasa | Me too.<br>It made me want to do the same... | talk |
| Tsukasa | And we'll need to keep doing our best in order to make that happen! | talk |

| Tsukasa | Developing an imagination on par with Bakuno's, and considering the story of a person's life... | talk |
| Tsukasa | As a first step, I've been trying to read novels with a more philosophical leaning than I'm used to... | talk |
| Tsukasa | But my mind hasn't been able to properly process the characters and their vastly differing values and ways of seeing the world. | talk |
| Tsukasa | And the depictions of characters' motivations feel quite nuanced and complex... As I'm reading, I find myself stopping to wonder why a character is acting a certain way. | talk |
| Rui | Hm... First, understanding someone with differing values from yourself is difficult even in one's real life. I believe that will require a slow and steady approach. | talk |
| Rui | As for the depictions of characters' motivations, I would chalk some of this up to you not being used to the genre, but... | talk |
| Rui | I think you may be finding it hard to process all the extra information that comes with more realistic characters. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Ah... That's a good point, since I've been thinking about each character's place in the world and their upbringing rather than focusing on what they're feeling in one particular scene. | talk |
| Rui | How about attempting an in-depth test run on a story that's a little more simple? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Something simple? | talk |
| Rui | Yes. Something with plenty of space between each line and only the minimum level of description required, but with pictures included to make it easy to fill in the blanks with your imagination... | talk |
| Rui | That is to say, a picture book. | talk |

| Rui | If you want to process vast amounts of information, you must first become adept at organizing it. | talk |
| Rui | A picture book is not likely to include personalities as complex as a philosophical novel, so it should make for the perfect way to practice your processing. | talk |
| Rui | As you just mentioned yourself, this is a long-term endeavor. I'm sure you'll find a method that works for you sooner or later... | talk |
| Rui | So until then, try to tackle this with patience, discipline and attention to detail. | talk |

| Rui | It seemed natural.<br>An honest thief who can't lie. A lovable criminal. That seemed right up your alley, Tsukasa. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, those very qualities are why I found this character so appealing! How can it not be fun to play him? | talk |
| Tsukasa | While we're on the subject, I have to ask... | talk |
| Tsukasa | The battle in the fog at the end... If you were the director, Rui, I'm guessing you would've wanted to use a snow machine. | talk |
| Tsukasa | On top of the fog greatly reducing visibility, the hero would have to deal with the cold while fighting off countless enemies. That's the kind of scene I feel you'd make it... | talk |
| Rui | Yes, that's not a bad idea. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Hah, I knew it! | talk |
| Tsukasa | That being said, the Rui Kamishiro I know would easily come up with something more spectacular than what I just said! | talk |
| Rui | Huh? | talk |
| Tsukasa | You always come up with ideas that I never expect, no? | talk |
| Rui | Thank you.<br>I appreciate the compliment. | talk |
| Rui | But you're also always exceeding my expectations with your performances, Tsukasa. | talk |
| Rui | Actually, not just you.<br>Emu and Nene as well. | talk |
| Tsukasa | I agree.<br>I believe Emu gave us a particularly big surprise as Ushiwakamaru this time around. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Oh, speaking of her, that reminds me... | talk |
| Tsukasa | I feel that she'd be the perfect person to play the enemy who appears during the middle of the fourth season of “The Phantom Assassin”! | talk |
| Rui | The happy-go-lucky sword master who was working as a mercenary? | talk |
| Rui | Yes, given Emu's innocent nature, she'd probably do a good job of capturing that character's enigmatic personality. | talk |
| Tsukasa | See what I mean?! | talk |
| Rui | Which means Nene would play the protagonist's assistant, yes? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, I agree!<br>She's clumsy in an endearing way, which I think makes her a good character. | talk |
| Tsukasa | And she undergoes a nice transformation. She starts out quiet, but slowly starts to help him after he saves her. | talk |
| Tsukasa | She's athletic and is the perfect partner when a big fight breaks out. | talk |
| Rui | We've become much more agile after we started training with Mikazukigumi, so I'm sure she'd do well when she and the protagonist fight alongside each other. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, we've all become much better at performing fight scenes thanks to them... | talk |
| Tsukasa | So, we could conceivably put on our own little historical drama someday! | talk |

| Tsukasa | Oh, doing it in SEKAI's an excellent idea! | talk |

| Tsukasa | It was made by the same director as “The Phantom Assassin”, so I felt that I needed to watch it! | talk |
| Rui | But I'm somewhat doubtful that it helped in terms of learning more about the genre. I mean, most of the early episodes are full of jokes and comedy. | talk |
| Tsukasa | True... I definitely wasn't expecting them to travel through time and find themselves in a school setting. I mean, they really tested the viewer's limits...! | talk |
| Tsukasa | But it grew on me as I watched.<br>Actually, I thought it was very good overall. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Oh, did you see that one movie where the heroes from the Phantom Assassin series join together in a grand battle...? | talk |
| Rui | Yes, that was incredible.<br>They did an excellent job including the best aspects of each season. Not only that... | talk |

| Rui | Interesting. Shinonome's one thing, but Haruka Kiritani? You managed to put together quite a team for this event. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, and I can't thank her enough.<br>I put her in a difficult position, knowing that she's an idol. | talk |
| Rui | So, have you already decided who is going to be doing what? I'm particularly interested in the water spider obstacle. | talk |
| Tsukasa | That's a part of the second stage, which we've assigned to Akito. | talk |
| Rui | Ah, Shinonome.<br>He seems like the right choice, with his core strength and sense of balance. | talk |
| Rui | Still, it's interesting how they decided to go with such an old-fashioned interpretation of the “water spider” device. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Hm? Old-fashioned? Didn't ninjas use those special shoes to walk across the water, just like the obstacle? | talk |
| Rui | Ah, so you haven't heard that one theory. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Theory...? | talk |
| Rui | The common interpretation is that ninjas used “water spider” floats to move just like water striders, yes. | talk |
| Rui | But one theory suggests that they didn't attach the floats to their feet at all.<br>Interesting, no? | talk |
| Tsukasa | What?!<br>Where did they put the floats, if not on their feet? | talk |
| Rui | On their bodies.<br>Either by wearing something like an inner tube, or by sitting on a plank of wood in the center. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Slow down.<br>Doesn't that mean submerging half your body?! | talk |
| Rui | Yes, the theory I read spoke of propelling yourself using your feet, while only your upper body remained above the surface. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Th-That's quite a revelation...<br>I honestly had no idea... | talk |
| Rui | Originally, people claimed that it would've been physically impossible for the traditional water spider to provide enough buoyancy to cross the water while standing. | talk |
| Rui | If anything, it only makes sense someone arrived on a more practical conclusion. | talk |
| Rui | However, it isn't wrong to believe that they also used these floats on their feet. | talk |
| Rui | For example, when crossing a swamp, they could have been used to disperse your weight in order to keep your feet from sinking. | talk |
| Rui | I have a feeling that they were used in different ways depending on the situation. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Y-Yes, I see... | talk |
| Tsukasa | I wasn't expecting to hear a theory that completely overturned my understanding of this subject! Again, I honestly had no idea! | talk |
| Rui | Yes, there was a brief period when this theory was on everyone's minds, but it faded into the annals of history not long after, without much impact at all. | talk |
| Rui | Perhaps it didn't find much purchase because, like you just said, Tsukasa... people didn't want their understanding of water spiders, or rather, their beliefs regarding ninjas to be shattered. | talk |
| Tsukasa | What do you mean? | talk |
| Rui | To keep that dream alive. | talk |
| Rui | A ninja running across the water like a real water spider is much cooler than the theory, no? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, true... | talk |
| Tsukasa | It may be physically impossible, but it's easy to think that perhaps ninjas could have still managed it. | talk |
| Rui | Also, Miku and the others have been working very hard to become ninjas after you told them that you'd be competing in Shinobi Battle. | talk |
| Rui | I'll actually be taking over some things that will allow them to experience being water spiders. | talk |
| Tsukasa | What?! Like the ones they had back then?! | talk |
| Rui | Oh, not exactly.<br>Mine are going to be simple imitations that use modern materials. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Still, that's incredible!<br>When will they be done? | talk |
| Rui | I have to order quite a lot of things, so even if I did put a rush on them, I probably won't finish making them until after Shinobi Battle. | talk |
| Rui | Which is a shame, since your team could've used them to practice... | talk |
| Tsukasa | Not a problem.<br>Our training will more than make up for that! | talk |
| Tsukasa | But I'd like to try what you make once you're done with them! | talk |
| Rui | Yes, of course. | talk |

| Nene | Huh? | talk |

| Tsukasa | Thank you for being so concerned about us! | talk |
| ArcLand Cast Member A | Haha. Hey, we're comrades who stood on the same stage together, aren't we? | talk |
| ArcLand Cast Member B | You really lit a fire under us during that exchange. | talk |
| ArcLand Cast Member A | And I'm sure this time will be no different. | talk |
| ArcLand Cast Member A | I mean, we get to see just how much you guys have all grown, right? | talk |

| Tsukasa | (Natalia Santi's “Living For The Stage”...) | talk |

| Tsukasa | (It includes everything from her process of getting into character to techniques of expression and bearing, and even vocal acting.) | talk |
| Tsukasa | (I see. This is what she was talking about the other day when we were discussing acting methods...) | talk |
| ??? | Tsukasa. | talk |

| Tsukasa | Hm...? | talk |
| Tsukasa | Whoa, Rui?! How long have you been there?! | talk |
| Rui | Hehe. That book must be very captivating. | talk |
| Rui | Mind if I ask what you're reading? | talk |
| Tsukasa | It's a book by Natalia Santi, one of the workshop teachers. | talk |
| Rui | Ah, I'm familiar with that one. | talk |
| Rui | It's received quite a lot of praise for its eminently practical approach to acting. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Yes, it should prove an invaluable tool while we attend this workshop. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Particularly because the material is so advanced. | talk |
| Tsukasa | The teachers are world-class, of course, and the participating members of ArcLand are seasoned veterans as well. | talk |
| Tsukasa | In almost every conversation, it's driven home how painfully shallow my understanding of the craft is. | talk |
| Rui | I see. So that's why you're studying up. | talk |
| Tsukasa | It sounds like things are hardly any easier over on the directors' course, either. | talk |
| Tsukasa | That's all you were thinking about during class today, right? Your attention was a thousand miles away. | talk |
| Rui | Your eyes are sharp. | talk |
| Rui | You're spot on, Tsukasa.<br>This assignment is proving quite a difficult one. | talk |
| Rui | It's not easy to change up one's entire approach to showmaking. | talk |
| Tsukasa | I see...<br>It sounds like we're both going to be wracking our heads for a while. | talk |
| Tsukasa | At the same time... It's thrilling, isn't it? | talk |
| Tsukasa | We've learned a lot through our training until now... | talk |
| Tsukasa | And the more we learn, the more we see we need to work on. | talk |
| Tsukasa | In other words, we still have so much room to grow. | talk |
| Tsukasa | So, I'm going to take this opportunity to grow once again, and get one step closer to becoming a star! | talk |
| Rui | Hehe. A typically Tsukasa-like positive outlook. | talk |
| Rui | But, you're right... This has been a fun challenge. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Oh, look at the time...<br>We've been talking so long that lunch is almost half over. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Let's hurry up and finish eating so that we're ready for classes this afternoon! | talk |
| Rui | Yes, you should probably hurry. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Hm? Me? What about you? Aren't you having lunch? | talk |
| Rui | I'll just stick with this drink.<br>I'd rather spend as much time as I can working on my show. | talk |
| Tsukasa | There you go again...! | talk |
| Tsukasa | If you don't eat properly, then you're going to struggle to come up with good ideas! | talk |
| Tsukasa | You really didn't bring anything?<br>If so, I'll share some of my food with you. Eat up. | talk |
| Rui | Alright...<br>I suppose I should listen to what my troupe leader has to say. | talk |
| Tsukasa | Well said.<br>So, let's eat! | talk |
